# captures.txt — the capture queue as a file (docs/QUEUE.md item 90,
# docs/DECISIONS.md entry 27).
#
# One stanza per capture, `key: value`, blank line between stanzas, `#` for a
# comment. Every key is a `longtrace.sh` input — the script's four positional
# arguments and the environment hooks documented in its header — so a stanza
# is a capture and nothing else needs writing to run one. `runqueue.sh` walks
# this file top to bottom, **one capture at a time**, and skips any stanza
# whose archive already exists, so it is re-runnable and a crash costs the
# capture in flight rather than the queue.
#
#   run:        the run number, and the archive's name
#   tag:        the archive's suffix
#   item:       the queue item this capture is owed to
#   why:        one line, for the run section this becomes in docs/ORACLE.md
#   frames:     the `!quit` frame
#   mapstyle:   14 Great Lakes, 18 East Indies
#   cfg:        the `-config` argument; `-` means none, and absent means the
#               default (`-config check.ini` on 14, none elsewhere)
#   end:        `[End Frame]` detail, setlog.py's `end:` list
#   start:      `[Start Game]` detail, setlog.py's `start:` list
#   dump_all:   DUMP_ALL, default 0
#   window:     "LO HI" for a DUMP_ALL window instead of the cheap dump
#   cover:      rontrace.cfg's body, default `cover=1`. `cover=1` runs on
#               this machine again since 2026-09-08 (run906; `docs/ORACLE.md`,
#               "Coverage is back"): the stubs write nothing after attach and
#               use no popad/popfd. Say `cover: cover=0` when the question is
#               only the draw stream — a coverage run passes every call
#               through a stub and is slower over a long run — and add
#               `cover: window=LO-HI` for per-frame sets.
#   cmd:        one `rontrace.cmd` line, repeatable, in file order
#   driver:     a script driven while the game runs, for the right-clicks the
#               cheat channel cannot issue — `move` is a teleport and **no
#               console command issues an order at all**
#   ffwd:       the `!ffwd` line; `-` means none. **Every stanza with a
#               `driver:` needs `-`** — under `!ffwd` the game stops drawing
#               and a click lands on a frozen picture (run47)
#   settle_min: bytes below which the poll loop will not call a run finished.
#               A thin `end:` needs it lowered — a per-frame block of a few
#               lines may never reach the 10 MB default, and the run would
#               then never be called settled
#   poll_max:   20-second polls before the loop gives up and kills the game
#               (default 160 = 53 minutes). **The give-up is a truncation,
#               not a stop**, so any capture that can run longer than its
#               bound must raise it. **Size it from the dumping, not the
#               run-up.** run53's stanza below says "this run is hours" and
#               takes 900 for 24,000 frames; that was the `cover=1` int3
#               forest, and run80 reached frame 23,960 of the same game in
#               **forty seconds** at `cover=0`. Every 900 in this file is on
#               a completed `cover=1` run where it was right — checked
#               2026-09-06, and no `cover=0` stanza carries one — but a new
#               stanza sized from run53's sentence would over-budget by two
#               orders. What costs wall-clock now is the window: run79 spent
#               fifty seconds on 6,910 frames of run-up and three minutes on
#               340 dumped ones
#   check:      a command run after the capture, from the repo root, to say
#               whether it landed; repeatable, and a non-zero exit is loud
#
# The order is the charter's: 108 first because it is the cheapest and fully
# specified, and it proves the pipeline before anything costly rides on it.
#
# **Run numbers start at 42.** 40 and 41 are taken — two undocumented
# `census` captures from 2026-08-30 that no run section in `docs/ORACLE.md`
# mentions. `longtrace.sh` archives its trace as `rontrace-run$N.log`
# unconditionally, so re-using a number silently overwrites another run's
# trace while leaving its gamelog in place, which is the worst of both. Check
# the Logs directory, not the documents, before picking a number.

run: 42
tag: islands-goodybucket
item: 108
why: the goody box's pile — bucket[2] stepping by 50 on frame 867
frames: 900
mapstyle: 18
# run39's own lobby: East Indies takes the profile's, never check.ini's.
cfg: -
# run39's detail exactly, plus LEADERS=2. The LEADERDATA block that carries
# `bucket`, `ages_get()` and `epoch_get(scan)` is the encrypted one, and
# `LeaderData::log_data@006e5110` announces it at detail **2** (the
# `this_00[1].handle = 2` before the `LeaderDataEncrypt::log_data` call at
# line 916) — not the 9 of the census, so this costs ~55 lines a leader a
# frame rather than 10,000.
end: MISC,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=2,DEATHS=1,LEADERS=2
start: MISC,WORLD=6,TERRAIN=2,GOODS=3,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=2,LEADERS=9,DEATHS=1

run: 44
tag: islands-turners
item: 96
why: a turning unit, which no capture has — guy_flags & 8 and the turn override
# `Guy::do_turn@005d97a0:15` swaps the standing walk for CHAR_TURN_LEFT/RIGHT
# on a guy whose piece names a turn, and no guy in any capture on disk carries
# the bit. The reading that makes this stageable is **which** call fires the
# override: `Unit::move_step` passes 1 only on its two turn-in-place branches,
# but `Guy::move:109` calls `turn_towards(this, des_angle, _, 1)` — and
# `turn_towards` passes that straight through to `do_turn` — on the standing
# arm, guarded only by `guy_flags & 2`, which every dumped guy has clear. So a
# turner unit **turning towards a target in a fight** fires it, which is far
# easier to stage than a 180-degree walk.
#
# The units are chosen off `unit_graphics.xml`: 274 of its 1,435 <UNIT> pieces
# name a CHAR_TURN_LEFT/RIGHT, across 40 base names, and CATAPULT, TREBUCHET
# and PIKEMEN are the three of them an Ancient-age game can be handed. Both
# sides get some, so the capture does not depend on either the AI's response
# or the human's units surviving.
frames: 700
mapstyle: 18
cfg: -
# GUYS=**4**, not run39's 2: `GuyData::log_data@005de6c0` announces levels 2,
# 3 and 4 and `cur_anim` sits past the last, so at 2 a GUY block stops after
# `ox` and the question cannot be asked of the file at all.
end: MISC,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=4,DEATHS=1,LEADERS=1
start: MISC,WORLD=6,TERRAIN=2,GOODS=3,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=4,LEADERS=9,DEATHS=1
# runwin.sh's tiles, which are known-good land beside the AI capital on this
# lobby (the AI starts on 198-214 x 201-214). A Quick Battle already starts at
# war, so no `war` line is needed — run23 is the null result that established
# it.
cmd: 150 add catapult who=1 203,207
cmd: 152 add catapult who=1 205,207
cmd: 154 add trebuchet who=1 203,210
cmd: 160 add hoplite who=0 206,210
cmd: 162 add hoplite who=0 206,208
cmd: 164 add pikemen who=0 204,212
cmd: 166 add catapult who=0 207,212
check: python3 tools/gamelog/cmdsran.py rontrace-run44.log 150:1 152:1 154:1 160:1 162:1 164:1 166:1
check: python3 tools/gamelog/turnanim.py gamelog-run44-islands-turners.txt

run: 45
tag: islands-groups
item: 23
why: does the AI's own play kill a mirrored group order — the XOR term, driver-free
# `Unit::kill_current_order@005e2cb0` assigns
# `group.facing = order.facing XOR reversing(leader.angle - order.angle)`,
# and GROUPS §13 says no run has fired the XOR term. `groupfacing.py` on
# run31 says why, exactly: its group move orders carry `facing 1` on frames
# 356-367 and the capture then stops, so the mirrored order is still alive
# when the log closes. The term needs that order to **die**.
#
# Before reaching for the mouse — the only path GROUPS §13 names, since the
# console's 102 commands include no formation verb and `move` is a teleport —
# this asks whether the AI does it unaided. run39's trace answers the half
# that can be answered without a capture: `Group::action_move_near`,
# `Form::compute` and `GroupData::find_leader` are all entered at frame 0 of
# a game with **no human input at all**, and `Unit::kill_current_order` at
# frame 1. So the machinery runs; what no dump on disk says is whether a
# *mirrored* layout is ever among the orders it kills, because no capture of
# an AI game has ever been taken with GROUPS=1.
#
# run39's lobby exactly, so `samegame.py --exclude GROUPDATA` can still say
# it is run39's game and it inherits run39's siblings.
mapstyle: 18
cfg: -
# **run31's detail exactly, and the reason is a trap that has now cost two
# runs.** `GROUPS=1` is what `GameLog::full_dump` gates `dump_groups` on, but
# turning it on is not enough: `GroupData::log_data@0045e1d0` never calls the
# Log vtable's `set_type`/`set_detail`, so its lines are accepted against
# whatever the **previous** dumper left behind — and `dump_deaths@0092fd80`
# ends by calling `WorldData::log_data` twice, leaving the current type at
# `WORLD`. With `WORLD=0` under `[End Frame]` the whole 512-slot pool is then
# dropped silently: GROUPS is on, `dump_groups` runs, and not one line
# survives `check_accept`. run30 hit it; so did this stanza's first attempt,
# which dumped the pool once in the start block (512 records) and never
# again. **`DEATHS` off under `[End Frame]`** is the fix — then the type
# `dump_groups` inherits is `UNITS`, set high enough to accept anything.
#
# The cost is that this is no longer run39's sibling by detail, so there is
# no `samegame.py` check here: at `UNITS=9` every frame differs for a reason
# that is not the game. The question is the XOR term, not sibling-hood.
end: MISC=9,UNITS=9,GROUPS=9,GUYS=9,LEADERS=1
start: MISC,WORLD=6,TERRAIN=2,GOODS=3,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=2,LEADERS=9,DEATHS=1,GROUPS=1
# run31 wrote 684 KB a frame at ~0.6 sim-frames a second, so this is seven
# times heavier than run39's line and about a quarter the speed. 900 frames
# rather than 1400 for that reason.
frames: 900
check: python3 tools/gamelog/groupfacing.py gamelog-run45-islands-groups.txt

run: 46
tag: greatlakes-fourthclick
item: 23
driver: tools/gamelog/clickdriver.sh
ffwd: -
why: a group marched back and forth, so a mirrored order is laid and then dies
# run45 settled that no AI lays a `GroupMoveOrder` — 945 in run31 against
# none in eight other archives, three of them DUMP_ALL windows taken during
# the AI's own fighting — so the record item 23 needs only exists behind a
# right-click. This is that click, three times, from `clickdriver.sh`.
#
# **Why three and not four.** An order of the move family dies when it is
# replaced *or when it completes*, and `Unit::kill_current_order` hands the
# mirror back either way. run31 stopped at frame 367 with its mirrored order
# still alive — its group move orders carry `facing 1` from 356 to 367, the
# last frame in the file — so what it lacked was not a fourth click but
# **time**. Click three lays the mirrored order; the 450 frames after it are
# what let that order die.
#
# **Why the destinations alternate.** `Unit::set_angle` toggles the flag when
# the leader is set to a heading 90 degrees or more off its own, so marching
# the group between two tiles on opposite sides of it makes every order after
# the first a ~180 degree turn and the toggle is not left to luck.
#
# Great Lakes rather than the islands: run32 placed buildings at tiles
# (6, 171) and (33, 161) on this lobby, so the corridor between them is known
# to be land, and a right-click that lands in water issues no order at all.
frames: 900
mapstyle: 14
# run31's detail, and DEATHS off under [End Frame] for the reason in run45's
# section: `dump_deaths` leaves the current type at WORLD and the pool is
# dropped silently.
end: MISC=9,UNITS=9,GROUPS=9,GUYS=9,LEADERS=1
start: MISC,WORLD=6,TERRAIN=2,GOODS=3,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=2,LEADERS=9,DEATHS=1,GROUPS=1

cmd: 152 add hoplite who=0 15,166

check: python3 tools/gamelog/groupfacing.py gamelog-run46-greatlakes-fourthclick.txt

run: 53
tag: greatlakes-24k-trace
item: 91
why: the word's ceiling on Great Lakes — the whole trace over 24,000 frames
# **The scout, not the measurement.** run10's capture is exhausted (item 114:
# 1772/1772, neither player parting, and run33's word past its end at 1802),
# so the next number on this map needs a longer game. This stanza takes the
# cheap half of item 91: the trace whole, and a `[End Frame]` thin enough that
# the dump is not the speed floor.
#
# What it buys is the **word** out to 24,000 — which is what says how big the
# expensive half has to be. run33 is 155 KB of dump per frame at frame 1,800
# and rising with the roster, so a run10-detail dump over 24,000 is GB-scale
# and most of its bytes would fall past wherever the word parts, where both
# sides are on streams of nobody's and the numbers are noise. Aim that run;
# do not guess it.
#
# `start:` stays run10's exactly, because `Initial` is what the harness stands
# the simulation up from and `run33_s_long_trace_says_where_the_word_parts`
# needs nothing else from the dump — the per-frame words are the trace's own
# FRAME records.
frames: 24000
mapstyle: 14
end: MISC
start: MISC,WORLD=6,TERRAIN=2,GOODS=3,UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,CITIES=5,GUYS=2,LEADERS=9,DEATHS=1
cover: cover=1
# The gamelog is now the start dump plus a thin per-frame block, so it will
# not pass the 10 MB default and would never be called settled.
settle_min: 500000
# 53 minutes is the default and this run is hours. The give-up path truncates
# rather than stops, so this is the difference between a capture and a waste.

# **run81 did not catch the unpack, and that is its finding.** It caught the
# whole approach and the reason: the merchant `1/19` reaches its deploy spot
# (32280, 36888) on block **6735** and the cast begins on **6736**, where
# `cur_anim` goes 8 -> 24, `end_time` 15 -> **149**, `stopped` 0 -> 1 and the
# `spell 656` order's `paid` 0 -> 1. `cur_time` then advances exactly one a
# frame — 1 on 6736, 64 on 6799, no gap, no reset — so the cast ends at
# 6736 + 148 = **6884**, and `unit_masks` is 9175050 on every one of run81's
# 70 blocks because the bit does not clear until then.
#
# So this is not a guess at a frame the way run77 and run78 were: it is a
# linear extrapolation off 64 measured samples of a counter that is dumped.
# The window opens 24 frames ahead of 6884 and closes 45 past it, which
# brackets the prediction either way and holds the deployed steady state
# long enough to see it settle into run76's shape (`special 6`,
# `gather_down` naming the good, `unit_masks` without `0x80000`).
#
# **`LEADERS=9` rides along, because the unpack's second half is the census.**
# `docs/ECONOMY.md` step 6: `Unit::do_gather` refuses a merchant while
# `unit_masks & 0x80000` is set, so the frame the bit clears is the frame the
# Citrus starts paying — `rare_owned`, the six rates, the population cap.
# That is the same event and it is only ever visible on this one frame of
# this one game. run80 measured `LEADERS=9` at ~1.7 MB a block, so 70 blocks

# **The overlap, which is the check with teeth, and the reason the window is
# twelve blocks wider than the hole.** `samegame.py` exits 0 when nothing
# differs — **including when nothing is in common**, which is a vacuous
# pass — so each of these asserts the common count and its exact range as
# well as the verdict, and `pipefail` carries samegame's own exit through
# the pipe. Six blocks each way, named. This is the claim the `rngcmp.py`
# line cannot make: the word is the LCG, not the state.
check: python3 tools/gamelog/samegame.py gamelog-run76-greatlakes-archermarch.txt gamelog-run83-greatlakes-wordwindow.txt | tee /dev/stderr | grep -qF "6 in common (6864..6869)"

# TAKEN 2026-09-06 — four minutes, 53 blocks, 22,451,274 bytes. All three
# checks ok: rngcmp 6931/6931 identical against run53, and **both overlaps
# 6 in common, 0 differing** — a state digest against each neighbour, which
# is what the word check cannot give. The hole holds one blocked stand and
# nothing else: no unit born, none dead, 77 units on all 53 blocks. `1/29`
# is blocked on sim-frame 6892 by the **standing citizen `1/17`** at
# (42360, 23736), and the shape is a **deflection, not a stop** — pushed
# back one step on 6893, then five frames sliding at -26 in x with y pinned
# at 23880, `idle` 0 throughout, diagonal resumed on 6899. Field lifetimes

tag: greatlakes-makelist
item: 238
why: Great Lakes' word parts at 6982 on a building this crate buys and the original does not — and the one dump that covers the frame carries no per-frame make-list
# **The frames are covered and the detail is not**, which is the one case
# where re-capturing an already-covered range is right. run79's window is
# [6910, 7250) and holds 6982 — but at `LEADERS=1` it carries **22
# `BEGIN MAKEOBJECT` records in the whole file**, which is the start dump
# and nothing per-frame. run80, at `LEADERS=9` over 41 blocks, carries
# **902** — exactly 22 a block. So the make-list is a `LEADERS=9` record and
# the divergence is a make-list question:
#
#     frame 6982: ours 7 theirs 5 — at 0,
#       ours   Leader::produce_building+0x1805
#       theirs Leader::make_stuff+0x221
#
# The falsifier this window is bought for: what player 1's make-list holds,
# frame by frame across 6982, and what `Leader::make_stuff` chooses from it —
# so "this crate queues a building the original does not" becomes a row that
# fails rather than a draw count that differs. `BUILDS=7` and `CITIES=5` stay
# in the detail because they name the building that gets queued, and the tail
# runs 47 frames past the parting so the choice's *consequence* is on disk
# too, not only the choice.
#
# **The window sits entirely inside run79's**, which makes the overlap check
# the whole window rather than six blocks. This is run83's practice taken to
# its limit: a window is priced by its blocks and the frames before it are
# free, so anchoring to a neighbour costs nothing and turns "same seed,
# therefore same game" into a digest. `LEADERS` is the **only** category
# that moves between the two stanzas, so `--exclude LEADERDATA` asks exactly
# the right question — is everything the two runs record in common identical.
# Checked before booking that the exclusion is enough: `LEADERS=9` does
# **not** double the top-level object records, so run80's "each object twice"
# note is about the *census block's own* nested records, which the exclusion
# removes with it. **That first measurement was loose and is corrected here**
# — the 127 it quoted was depth-2 `UNITDATA` (87) plus the `UNITDATA` nested
# inside `ANIMALDATA` (40), two record kinds added together. Measured
# properly by depth: run80's block 23960 has **87 depth-2 `UNITDATA`, 87
# distinct `(who, o)`, none duplicated**, and at `LEADERS=9` each frame's
# four `LEADERDATA` blocks gain `DIPLOMACY` x32, `MAKEOBJECT` x22, `SITE`
# x20 and `PERSONALITY` x4 where at `LEADERS=1` they nest nothing at all.
# The conclusion stands and this run's own check is the proof of it.
# `docs/ORACLE.md`, run80, amended 2026-09-06.
frames: 7045
mapstyle: 14
# run79's detail with LEADERS 1 -> 9, and nothing else moved.

# **What each row would falsify.**
#
# * **Block 7807, `1/6`.** This crate steps to (39699, 38795); the trace
#   says the original is *blocked* on that frame. If the original's `1/6`
#   is still on (39720, 38808) there, the word is the step and the fault is
#   in f7805's turn-without-step after the waypoint pop — §8.5's candidate,

#   **refused** and the stand belongs to a unit or a figure this reading
#   never named.

#   waypoint pop and the turn. If the original spends one block there, the
#   frame is lost in the *turn* and `Guy::move`'s heading test is the row;
#   if it spends three, the pop is early here.
# * **Blocks 7811-7813, `1/7`.** This crate sets §6 step 5's **wait** flag

#   7813. The trace says the original spends a `SITE_PAUSE` on 7810 and this
#   crate spends none. If the original's `1/7` carries a non-zero `pause`
#   on block 7811, the roll on that frame is `1/7`'s and the
#   **wait-versus-repath predicate is wrong** — which is the shape
#   `docs/audit/README.md` says the errors take. If its `pause` is 0 there,
#   the missing roll is the frame offset carried forward and the predicate
#   stands.
# * **Block 7803, `1/7`, `pause 3`.** No dump on this disk has ever printed
#   a non-zero `MOVEORDER pause` — every one of run88's 1,467 is 0 — so
#   `docs/COLLISION.md` §9's standing row ("the `% 9 + 1` itself rests on

#   it.

#   emptied, parked on (39666, 38676) for the remaining 86 blocks. A
#   different arrival block makes the residue a build-arrival question and
#   not a collision one.
# * **Blocks 7790-7802.** Both sides agree here or the whole framing is
#   wrong: the trace says everything below 7806 agrees, and ten of those
#   blocks are run88's own and already byte for byte.

# **The teeth, and neither of them is run85's `collide_frame` transition.**

# `end:` category, and the window's own extent, which is what a truncation
# or a mis-landed `frame_window` breaks. Two fields from two categories, so
# a downgrade of either is caught.

# **run45's detail exactly, and the reason is the trap that has cost three
# runs.** `GROUPS=1` is what `GameLog::full_dump` gates `dump_groups` on,
# but `GroupData::log_data@0045e1d0` never calls the Log vtable's
# `set_type`/`set_detail`, so its lines are accepted against whatever the
# **previous** dumper left — and `dump_deaths@0092fd80` ends by calling
# `WorldData::log_data` twice, leaving the type at `WORLD`. With `WORLD=0`
# under `[End Frame]` the whole pool is dropped silently. **`DEATHS` off**
# is the fix: the type `dump_groups` then inherits is `UNITS`, set high

# **This assertion was written as run80's 41 at 23960..24000 and failed on the

# by exactly one entry each way (`ATTACKORDER`, `GROUPATTACKTOORDER`, both

# block against 90. So the extra bytes are *inside* the records: the ini value

# settings. (**This comment first said run19 was a `DUMP_ALL` window and that
# was wrong** — a real `DUMP_ALL` block is 50-64 MiB, which is why those exist

# run110 — chapter one's group pool at the golden word (item 399, 2026-09-19)
#
# **Not a runqueue stanza: no `run:` key, on purpose.** Chapter one runs on
# the golden lane (`viadriver.sh` + `tools/explore/golden_capture.sh`), which
# `runqueue.sh` cannot drive, so this block is the predictions and the checks
# written down *before* the run — the discipline run107's stanza carries —
# and nothing here is a `longtrace.sh` input. The command is:
#
#   zsh tools/gamelog/viadriver.sh tools/explore/golden_capture.sh \
#       ~/ron-golden/g5 --map 14 --end-frame 640 --log-window 610 630 \
#       --detail end:MISC=9,UNITS=9,GROUPS=9,GUYS=2,LEADERS=1 \
#       --detail misc:COMMANDMANAGER=1 \
#       --cmd-file tools/gamelog/golden/chapter1.cmd
#
# **DEATHS is off under `[End Frame]` and that is the whole trap**
# (`docs/ORACLE.md`, "The group pool is a per-frame record"):
# `GroupData::log_data@0045e1d0` sets no type of its own, and
# `dump_deaths@0092fd80` ends on two `WorldData::log_data` calls, so with
# `DEATHS` on the pool is accepted against `WORLD`'s threshold and vanishes
# silently. With `DEATHS` off the type `dump_groups` inherits is `UNITS`,
# raised to 9 here. `full_dump@00930380`'s dispatch order is the proof:
# `0xd` UNITS, then `0xf` WALLS and `0x10` AMMO (both 0), then `0x11` DEATHS,
# then `0x12` GROUPS.
#
# **`ARMY` is NOT an `[End Frame]` category and the brief's wording is wrong
# about that.** There is no `ARMY` key in `gamelog.ini`, `GameLog::dump_armies
# @0092fc50` has no caller in the export, and `full_dump`'s per-key list has
# no armies index: `ArmiesData::log_data` is reached only from
# `dump_all@0092f2d0`, i.e. `DUMP_ALL=1` — run25-27's shape, ~70 MB a frame.
# So this capture takes the half that is cheap and decisive, and the ARMY
# half is booked only if the group pool does not settle it.
#
# **Why it is owed.** Every chapter-one capture on disk (`~/ron-golden/{g0..g4,
# c1,h1,t1,probe1}`, `docs/RUNS.md` run101-run108) runs `UNITS=3,BUILDS=7,
# CITIES=5,GUYS=2,LEADERS=2,DEATHS=1` and **no `GROUPDATA` at all** — checked
# on g4's block 618, whose record list is ANIMALDATA, ATTACKORDER, BUILDDATA,
# BUILDQUEUE, CITIES, CITY, EXPLORETOORDER, FRAME, GATHERORDER, GUY,
# LEADERDATA, MOVEORDER, OBJECT, PATHDATA, STACK, SUBOBJECT, TARGETORDER,
# UNITDATA, UNITORDER, WALLDATA, WORLD. The five Great Lakes/islands archives
# that do carry a pool are different games.
#
# **What the widening of UNITDATA already established, and it is new.**
# who=1 holds **two** groups at every block 616..626, not one: `1/6`, `1/7`,
# `1/8` carry `group 64` with `form_mod -1`, and `1/0` — who=1's explorer,
# standing at (40424, 11725), a cell from this crate's own muster centre
# (38784, 13440) — carries `group 65` with **`form_mod 50`**, a live
# formation slot. This crate has one group for who=1 (army 0, three
# hoplites) and the explorer in none. So the group topology differs before
# any question about `Army::process` is asked.
#
# **The decisive field is `GroupData::army` (+0x8), which `log_data` prints**
# (line 91 of the export), and the second is `order_num` (+0x2c), which every
# `Group::action_*` steps.
#
# **What is expected to PART — and one of these two must.**
#
# - **`group 64`'s `army` is −1.** Then the hoplites are in no army, nothing
#   the emergency ticks reaches them, and this crate's `Unit::add_to_army`
#   join at 615 (`docs/ARMY.md` §4.2's `think_attack` head) is the bug — the
#   army's own group is 65, the explorer's, which is where `form_mod 50` and
#   the muster centre both point.
# - **or `group 64`'s `army` is 0 and its `order_num` does not step at 618.**
#   Then membership is right and `Army::process`'s emergency tick took a
#   branch that issues nothing — `do_mustering`'s `status = 2` into
#   `do_marching`'s `find_target` miss (`status = 8`, return), or a status
#   without the `0x10` forming bit this crate carries from birth.
#   `order_num` on blocks 618, 619 and 620 separates them.
#

#

#   616 on, and `group 65` `num 1`, `who 1`, `list` = 0. If the pool does not

tag: greatlakes-ammolaunch

why: the seventh draw on Great Lakes 9451 is a launch offset, and `AmmoData::log_data` prints the arrow's own `sx, sy, sz` — so `(sx - guy.x, sy - guy.y)` is measured rather than derived and no `.bh3` skeleton reader is needed
# **THE DISK WAS GREPPED FIRST, and it half-answers the question — which is
# why this stanza's detail is not the one the item asked for.**
#
# Two archives DO carry ammo records: **run17 (173 blocks)** and run29 (1).
# `docs/QUEUE.md` 396 and `docs/COMBAT.md` §20.3 both say no dump on this
# disk has `AMMO` enabled; the sweep behind that sentence looked for
# `AMMODATA`, and the block's name is **`AMMO`**. It is string-table index
# **127** — `AmmoData::log_data@00679c00` passes `int_str_array + 0x9ec` to
# `Log::begin`, the table's stride is a 20-byte `String`, and 0x9ec/20 = 127
# indexes `Data/internal_strings.xml`, where `UNITDATA` 7086, `OBJECT` 142,
# `BUILDDATA` 287, `GUY` 3940, `LEADERDATA` 4627, `WALLDATA` 7128 and
# `ANIMALDATA` 131 all land on their own names. Seven for seven, so 127 is
# `AMMO` and not a guess.
#
# **What run17 gives for nothing.** 24 shots from three Slingers (type 82),
# each with `sx, sy, sz` and, through the enclosing `UNITDATA`, the shooter's
# guy position. Rotating `(sx-guy.x, sy-guy.y)` back by the guy's own angle
# (`theta = angle / 2^32 * 2pi`, CCW) collapses all 24 onto **three**
# model-space vectors:
#
#     dz 151   (+39.44, -118.04)   |m| 124.45   18 shots
#     dz 177   (-42.88, -112.29)   |m| 120.20    4 shots
#     dz 178   (+68.21,  -68.15)   |m|  96.43    2 shots
#
# each held to +-2, which is the integer quantisation of `sx` and `guy.x`.
# So §20.3's model — `get_position` returns a per-(piece, node, anim,
# starttime) entry and rotates it by the guy's facing — is **confirmed on
# the disk before this run**, and what is missing is only the Longbowman's
# own entries and the animation that picks them: run17's `GUY` detail is 1,
# so it prints no `cur_anim`.
#
# **`AMMO=5`, not the item's `AMMO=1`.** `AmmoData::log_data` opens level 1
# for `cur_time/total_time/who/o/whom/ox` and **level 2 for `sx, sy, sz, ex,
# ey, ez, angle`** — at `AMMO=1` the launch point is silently dropped, which
# is `docs/ORACLE.md`'s "The detail level is the knob" exactly. 5 takes the
# whole record (`traj`, `v1z`, `dx`, `flags`, `gpiece`, `graph_index`,
# `splash_area`, `index`, `num_guys`, `accuracy`) because the rule is to diff
# the whole record, and an ammo block is a few dozen lines with at most four
# arrows alive.

# attack 1..4). Each unit fires **twice per thirty-frame cycle**, which is
# `AMMO_PER_ATT 2`, so A and C are two different release events of one anim.

# run17 settles the lifetime with no ambiguity: an ammo with `total_time T`

# during the block where `cur_time` would be T. §20.1's farm takes damage on
# blocks 9452 (twice), 9465 and 9470, so the original's own are

#

#   run17's three Slinger families are 124.5/151, 120.2/177 and 96.4/178.
#   Anything outside that says the Longbowman's skeleton is nothing like the
#   Slinger's and the transfer below is void; the measurement still stands.
# - A and C are one anim at two `starttime`s and so **may differ**; B and D
#   likewise. Four entries, not two — and if A and C come back identical,

# copy of it when one is held.

# stand as they were):
# **P3 — the bit.** S's probe (`detect_unit_collision this=1/62

# reads — the copy if one is printed, else the live one.

#     census under it says whether any unit claims it.
